2026年 10月 30日 全7頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月鉱工業生産 
自動車工業が挽回生産で持ち直し、生産全体は3カ月ぶりに上昇

経済調査部 エコノミスト ビリング 安奈

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 9月の生産指数は前月比＋2.2％と、コンセンサス（同＋1.2％、Bloomberg調
査）を上回り、3 カ月ぶりに上昇した。自動車工業は挽回生産で持ち直したほか、AI
関連需要で生産用機械工業なども増産し、生産全体を押し上げた。経済産業省は基調
判断を「一進一退」に据え置いた。 
◼ 先行きの生産指数は緩やかに上昇する見込み。AI関連需要などが引き続き国内生産を
押し上げよう。ただし、金利高による海外需要の下振れなどが生産を下押しするリス
クには注意が必要だ。 
◼ 2026年 11月 9日に公表予定の 9月分の景気動向指数は、先行 CIが前月差▲0.9ptの
117.1、一致CIが同＋0.7ptの 119.5と予想する。この予測値に基づくと、9月の基調
判断は機械的に「改善」に据え置かれる。

2 / 7

【生産】自動車工業は挽回生産で持ち直し、AI関連需要で生産用機械工業も増産 
2026年 9月の生産指数は前月比＋2.2％と、コンセンサス（同＋1.2％、Bloomberg調査）を
上回り、3カ月ぶりに上昇した。経済産業省は基調判断を「一進一退」に据え置いた。 
生産指数を業種別に見ると、15業種中 8業種が前月から上昇した。自動車工業（前月比＋
5.5％）は 2カ月ぶりに上昇した。品目別では、普通乗用車（同＋3.3％）や駆動伝導・操縦装
置部品（同＋7.0％）などが増産となった。前月は熊本地震や台風による工場稼働停止や部品
供給の停滞を受けて生産が落ち込んだが、9 月は生産体制がおおむね正常化したことで挽回生
産が進み、自動車工業の生産は持ち直した。また、生産用機械工業（同＋3.4％）は4カ月連続
で上昇した。品目別では、AI関連製品である半導体製造装置（同＋13.8％）のほか、化学機械
（同＋34.9％）などが増産となった。経済産業省「鉱工業出荷内訳表」によると、生産用機械
工業は国内向け（同＋7.8％）が好調だった。汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋4.8％）では、コン
ベヤ（同＋142.0％）や運搬用クレーン（同＋35.5％）が増産となり、2カ月ぶりの上昇となっ
た。一方、鉄鋼・非鉄金属工業（前月比▲1.6％）や窯業・土石製品工業（同▲1.9％）など 7
業種は前月から低下した。鉄鋼・非鉄金属工業では亜鉛めっき鋼板（同▲10.0％）などが、窯
業・土石製品工業ではガラス繊維（短繊維）（同▲6.3％）などが減産となった。 
財別では、生産財（前月比＋1.0％）、耐久消費財（同＋5.3％）、非耐久消費財（同＋0.9％）、
建設財（同＋2.5％）、資本財（除．輸送機械） （同＋1.0％）のいずれも増産となった。設備投
資の関連指標である資本財（除．輸送機械）はAI需要を背景に大幅な増産が見込まれる。

【出荷・在庫】出荷指数は自動車工業などを中心に2カ月ぶりに上昇 
2026年 9月の出荷指数は前月比＋0.6％と 2カ月ぶりに上昇した。業種別では、自動車工業
（同＋2.3％）や汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋5.5％）など 15業種中 8業種が上昇した。財別に
見ると、非耐久消費財と資本財（除．輸送機械）、建設財は低下した一方、耐久消費財と生産
財は上昇した。在庫指数は同＋2.5％、在庫率指数は同＋2.0％だった。

図表 2：鉱工業の生産・出荷・在庫（左）と財別の生産（右）

生産予測調査による。

【先行き】生産指数は緩やかに上昇する見込み 
先行きの生産指数は、緩やかな上昇基調を辿るとみている。AI関連需要などが引き続き国内
生産の押し上げ要因となる。ただし、世界的な金利高、特に米国の金利高による海外需要の下
振れなどが生産を下押しするリスクには注意が必要だ。 
AI関連財の拡大は 2024年から日本国内の生産を押し上げてきており、今後も生産の押し上
げ要因になると見込まれる1。26年 8月の世界半導体販売額（3カ月平均）を見ると、前年比＋
144.3％と強い伸びが続いている。米国の販売額（同＋180.9％）がけん引する中、日本（同＋
62.3％）2も力強さを示している。また、日本半導体製造装置協会によると、26年9月の半導体
製造装置販売高（3カ月平均）は同＋48.2％と高い伸びとなった3。AI関連を中心とする旺盛な
半導体需要が反映されているとみられる。ただし、米国の金利高でAI関連需要が下振れする可
能性には注意が必要だ。高金利下で米企業の資金調達負担が増え、多額の資金を必要とする AI
関連投資が鈍化すれば4、日本の生産も輸出減を通して下押しされるリスクがある。 
なお、中東情勢による悪影響は引き続き注視する必要がある。米国の中間選挙後に対イラン
攻撃が再開される可能性も報じられており5、原油供給をめぐる不安は引き続き残る。一方、今
年 2月末に始まった中東危機を受け、ホルムズ海峡の輸送停滞への対応として政府が国家備蓄
原油を放出して以降、初めて買い戻しが実施された6。代替調達が進み、国家備蓄原油の取り崩
しが一服し、備蓄の補充が始まったことは好材料だ。ただし、情勢が再び悪化すれば、原材料
不足を通じて生産が下押しされる可能性がある。 
製造工業生産予測調査では、10月の生産指数は前月比＋5.1％と見込まれる。業種別では 11
業種中 9業種が上昇する見通しだ。電子部品・デバイス工業（同＋16.6％）で集積回路などの
増産が計画されているほか、生産用機械工業（同＋15.5％）では半導体・フラットパネルディ
スプレイ製造装置など、AI関連製品の増産が全体を押し上げる見通しだ。生産指数全体の計画
のバイアスを補正した試算値（最頻値）7で見ると、10月は同＋3.9％と上昇が見込まれる。 
11月の生産指数は前月比＋0.8％と見込まれている。業種別では 11業種中 7業種が上昇する
見通しだ。輸送機械工業（同＋4.3％）では乗用車など、汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋4.8％）
では計測・分析機器などの増産が見込まれている。

2026年6月23日）を参照。

（2026年10月 23日） 
4 詳細は、矢作大祐・藤原翼「米国経済見通し CoreWeave問題をどうみるか」（大和総研レポート、2026年

【26年9月景気動向指数】先行CIは低下、一致CIは上昇を見込む 
鉱工業指数の結果を受けて、2026年 11月 9日に公表予定の 9月分の景気動向指数は、先行
CIが前月差▲0.9ptの 117.1、一致 CIが同＋0.7ptの 119.5と予想する（図表 4） 。先行CIで
は構成指標のうち、鉱工業用生産財在庫率指数（逆サイクル）や最終需要財在庫率指数（逆サ
イクル）、東証株価指数などが前月から悪化した。一致 CIでは構成指標のうち、生産指数（鉱
工業）や鉱工業用生産財出荷指数、有効求人倍率（除学卒）などが改善した。この予測値に基
づくと、2026年9月の基調判断は機械的に「改善」に据え置かれる。

図表 4：景気動向指数（先行 CI、一致 CI）と基調判断の推移

（注）左図の直近は大和総研による予測値。シャドーは景気後退期。2026年9月の基調判断は大和総研予想。

3Q largely in-line; mix-driven margin dilution is well understood — Sinbon 
reported 3Q26 EPS of NT$3.79 (+1% q/q and +24% y/y), broadly in line with our 
estimate and 4% below consensus. Revenue rose 8% q/q and +28% y/y to 
NT$9.7bn, topping our/BBG forecast 4%/2%. GPM decreased 77bps to 23.8% vs. 
our estimate of 24.6%, primarily reflecting rising semi cabinet assembly sales and 
increased shipments of auto and communications. We believe the market is well 
aware of this mix-driven dilution. Opex remained at an elevated level due to 
increased product development and sampling, alongside higher freight expenses. 
Operating profits +50% y/y, missing our/BBG estimates 4%/7%, respectively.  
Solid topline momentum into 2027E led by semi — We expect semi cabinet 
assembly to remain the key growth driver, with sales potentially increasing to NT$5–
5.5bn in 2027E from NT$3–3.5bn in 2026E, led by solid demand from two major 
semi customers. While increasing contribution from this business could dilute group 
GPM, it should support sales and absolute gross profit growth. Emerging 
applications including humanoid robots and drones should provide additional

contributions as shipment volume scales. These opportunities should complement

Implications — We believe the margin implications of a rising semi sales mix are well
understood by the market and leave our investment thesis intact. Our constructive
view is based on steady growth in existing businesses, supplemented by medium- to
long-term opportunities in humanoid robots, drones, and LEO satellites. We also

view Sinbon as a relatively defensive stock amid AI-related market volatility, given
its limited direct AI exposure and diversified business portfolio. The shares could
benefit from investor rotation toward companies with resilient underlying
fundamentals, while emerging applications provide additional support for the
longer-term growth outlook.

### Page 4

Sinbon (3023.TW)
26 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Sinbon
Valuation
Our TP for Sinbon is NT$370. We value Sinbon on PE to capture the company’s earnings growth prospects. We set our target
PE at 23x, which we set at a 20% premium to the historical average of 19x since 2018. We believe 23x is justified in view of
improving fundamentals with strong momentum of industrials segment, driving a clear margin expansion and solid earnings
growth in 2026E. Applying this to our 2H26-1H27E EPS forecast, we arrive at our target price of NT$370.

Risks
Key downside risks that could cause Sinbon's share to deviate from our target price include 1) slower-than-expected growth in
non-IT/non-consumer applications; 2) Intensified competition; 3) Raw material price hike and labor cost increases; 4) FX
volatility; 5) Greater-than-expected impact from the geopolitical conflict.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned
to a Catalyst Watch Upside, Catalyst Watch Downside, STV Upside, or STV Downside call is considered Catalyst Watch/STV
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 3

Unimicron (3037.TW)
28 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Long-term strategies: Resources concentrated on ABF/ AI PCB — As we
highlighted, Unimicron has been converting BT capacity into ABF, which currently
causes some BT capacity to be idle during the transition period (negative impact will
likely last into 1H27, in our view). Thus, currently, we are less bullish on its BT GM
recovery in the near term vs other BT peers. However, we expect this conversion will

help the company to secure more ABF demand earlier considering customers
currently are still waiting for those ABF greenfield capacity expansion projects to
come in the next few years. For HDI/mSAP , Unimicron would only focus on AI
server/optical module products in the long term. To note that, the company is still
less aggressive in HDI capacity expansion given higher competition from Chinese
peers. Meanwhile, the company is also downsizing its traditional PCB capacity (i.e.,

for non-AI business) and just announced its plan to sell its PCB plant in Japan to
other players. We expect there will be more downsizing plans to come for traditional
PCB capacity.
GM expansion on track; reiterate Buy — We raise our 2026/27 /28E by 10%/5%/5%
to factor in robust GM expansion by ABF. We remain positive on its GM expansion by
ABF and also see potential upside coming from HDI and BT segments. We raise our
DCF-based TP to NT$1,550 (30x 2027E EPS) from NT$1,500 and reiterate our Buy

rating.

### Page 6

Unimicron (3037.TW)
28 October 2026 Citi Research
6
Unimicron
Company description
Established in 1990, Unimicron is the world's No.1 PCB maker and HDI board
maker. After the acquisition of PPT in late 2009, its revenue exposure to
substrate has surpassed 40%, with the rest almost equally shared by
conventional PCB and HDI. Unimicron has manufacturing sites in Taiwan and
China (Suzhou, Kunshan, Shenzhen). UMC is the largest shareholder, with a
~13% stake.

Investment strategy
We rate Unimicron shares a Buy. Our positive view is premised on: more
upside potential in the ABF segment thanks to product-mix improvements,
better pricing on more complex product upgrade, and capacity expansion.
Despite macro uncertainty, from a long-term perspective, we believe the
tightness in ABF is unlikely to ease without much new capacity ramped
aggressively in the upcoming years, and Unimicron should be the largest
beneficiary with its scale in ABF. Thanks to AI GPU/ASIC demand, we now see

strong growth in 2026/27E ABF consumption. We believe Unimicron could
see strong revenue growth again as it did in 2019–21 from both ABF new
capacity and a BT rebound.
Valuation
Our target price for Unimicron is NT$1,550. We use a 1-year forward-looking
DCF-based valuation (ie, end-2027) to reflect Unimicron's ability to generate
stable cash. With a risk-free rate of 5.0% (10-year government bond rate), a
market risk premium of 7%, and an equity beta of 1.43 (2-year weekly share

price), we calculate Unimicron's WACC as 14.6%. Our target price is equivalent
to 62x/30x 2026/27E EPS, which is higher than the +1std level of consensus
PE multiple of the past five years. However, as the market focuses on 2027
upcycle, we believe valuation on 2027E EPS is still in line with historical peak
valuations of 25–30x during the last upcycles.
Risks
Key downside risks that could mean Unimicron shares trade below our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected HDI business; 2) significant increase

in ABF supply in the industry; and 3) stronger-than-expected margin pressure
from depreciation due to higher capex investments.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered

stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also

suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in

exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility

or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be
different from and does not affect a stock’s fundamental equity rating, which reflects a longer-term total absolute return

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113) 金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

曜越(3540)：2027 年 Gaming PC 與 AI 工 
建議：評等買進

一、曜越為電競電腦零組件大廠，積極切入AI 高效能工
作站系統 
曜越科技股份有限公司成立於 1999 年 11 月，為電腦機
殼、散熱器(模組)及電源供應器製造商，以自有品牌
Thermaltake 銷售桌上型電腦散熱模組、機殼、電源供
應器，專攻 clone 遊戲玩家市場，近年來公司積極整合
機殼、散熱、電源與系統配置能力，從單一產品銷售延
伸至完整 Gaming PC 系統解決方案，滿足高階 
Gaming PC 的效能與穩定性需求。4Q26 公司將切入
AI 工作站(AI Workstation)新業務 ，拓展地端 AI 運算
市場。2026 年 1~9 月主要產品營收占比，Gaming PC 
35%、電源供應器 33%、機殼 14%、散熱 13%、其他
5%。

二、2Q26 本業疲弱，EPS 0.01 元 
曜越 2Q26 營收 10.16 億元，年減 20.5%，毛利率則從
前期 28.1%升至 30.7%，主要是因記憶體漲價，使毛利
率較佳的客製化裝機業務佔比提升， 2Q26 稅後淨利
96.8 萬元，EPS 0.01 元。 
三、曜越從 PC 零組件品牌轉型為系統整合品牌，並積
極切入 AI 工作站市場  
展望下半年 ，3Q26 營收年減 2.9%， 估計3Q26 稅後 EPS 
0.09 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上 AI 工作站從雲端到
地端的基本條件已於 2026 年第三季成熟，產品規劃完
成，第四季開始進入市場，出貨量將逐步上升。 
2026 年前九月 Gaming PC 營收佔比已達 35%， 超越電
源供應器的 33%，成為最大產品線，象徵系統整合業務
已成為營收核心。曜越的電競 PC 分為三個價格層次：
入門級 1,599-1,799 美元、進階級 2,599 美元、高階
4,399-4,499 美元。其中核心零組件如機殼、散熱、電源
皆是自製，而主機板、CPU、GPU、SSD 等為外購，透
過美國、德國、澳洲、中國等海外子公司進行在地組裝
與銷售。展望下一階段，曜越將複製電競 PC 的成功模
式，推出五款 AI 工作站與 AI PC 產品線，從入門級 AI
電競 PC（View 380 XL，1,801 TOPS）到企業旗艦雙
系統 AI 工作站（A1500 Dual，16,000 TOPS） ，搭載
NVIDIA RTX 5090、RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 及 AMD 
Radeon AI PRO R9700 等高階 GPU。AI 工作站從雲端
到地端的基本條件已於 2026 年第三季成熟，產品規劃
完成，第四季開始進入市場，2027 年營收貢獻將逐季顯
現 ， 目標2027 年占整體業績一成 ， 主要鎖定AI 開發者、
專業創作者、學術科研及中小企業等應用。這代表曜越
正式從傳統電競跨足企業級 AI 運算、專業開發與數位
創作的地端商機，由於商用工作站毛利通常優於一般消
費級產品，這將是明年拉升獲利結構與擴展新市場的最
大看點。 
四、評等買進理由 
投顧認為曜越已從機箱、電源供應器及散熱等 PC 零組
件，轉型至拓展更高毛利率的電競PC 系統整合與 AI 工
作站業務，使 2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 30%以上，
預期 2027 年營收將成長 15%，估計 2026 年 EPS 0.46
元，2027 年 EPS 1.95 元，給予買進的建議，目標價31
元。(16x2027EPS)。

研 究  報 告                
上品(4770.TT) 
產業：化學工業 
AI 需求帶動訂單攀升，擴產與高毛利產品推升獲利成⻑  
 投資評價與建議 
考量AI驅動全球半導體擴產 ， 帶動氟素設備 、 化學品槽⾞與建廠⼯程訂單成⻑ ，
且低毛利舊單消化、高毛利產品放量及兩岸產能擴充推升毛利率，參考同業日
本AGC、Daikin PE區間13-21倍，基於2027年EPS給予17倍PER。 
 重點評論分析 
AI需求推升訂單動能 ：3Q26單季營收季增22.5%至16.0億元 ， 受惠AI帶動國內
晶圓廠客戶持續建廠，推升氟素內襯設備與管道系統的需求，年初在手訂單38
億元陸續轉為實質營收貢獻，且低毛利訂單已消化完畢，預期在高毛利訂單出
貨下，推估毛利率進⼀步提升1.38ppts至37%，稅後EPS 4.46元，2026全年營
收年增18%至55.8億元，毛利率提升3.6ppts至36.58%，稅後EPS 14.45元。 
擴產與高階新品推升獲利成⻑ ：2027年受惠兩岸產能擴充及高單價槽⾞ 、 建廠
設備進入交貨高峰 ， 2026年槽⾞接單量已超越去年的2倍突破1000台 ， 台灣廠
月產能將由80台提升至140台，並規劃進⼀步擴至170台，中國嘉興廠亦將提
升產能20%，預計自1Q27起挹注營收，產品方面，高溫環境用離型膜、高純
度配件及過濾產品將隨客戶認證逐步放量 ， 帶動高毛利產品比重提升 ， PTFE低
介電材料則仍處配方測試階段，具中⻑期成⻑潛⼒，受惠在地化供應、⼯程整
合服務及產能利用率提升 ， 預估2027年營收年增25%至69.7億元 ， 毛利率提升
2.92ppts至39.5% ，費用率因非大幅建廠 ， 維持在11.8% ， 稅後EPS 19.84元 。  
 投資風險 
客戶擴產進度延遲、高階新品開發進度不如預期、原料成本壓抑獲利表現。

| 公司簡介  
主要進口上游高階氟素樹脂 ， 如 PFA 、 PTFE 原料 ， 並自行加⼯為半成品的板材與管材 ， 以及製造終端高階等設備 ，
核心產品包括內襯設備(桶槽、反應釜)、內襯管道與管件、化學品運輸槽⾞以及⼯程服務等，這些產品主要應用於
先進半導體製程，包含 2nm 以下，與高純度電子級化學品，如硫酸、氫氟酸、光阻劑等的生產、儲存、輸送與廢
液回收系統，由於氟素材料具有極佳的耐酸鹼腐蝕與抗污染特性，設備能將金屬離子控制在 PPT 等級以下、顆粒
控制在 0.02 以下，為晶圓代⼯與特化廠維持極高良率與穩定品質的關鍵所在。 
截至 2Q26 產品別營收，內襯設備佔比 68.3%，為公司最核心的營收來源，包含特化廠與晶圓廠建廠所需的整套
重化線純化槽、儲罐、反應釜及槽⾞等設備，其他材料與半成品 16.7%，主要為第⼀階段加⼯的氟素板材與管材
半成品，多數留作自用，部分出口給海外(日本、歐洲)缺乏加⼯業者的獨立市場，內襯管道與管件 9.6%，用於連
結各機台設備與廠區內的化學品輸送管線系統，⼯程業務 5.4%，協助電子化學品廠內部的襯裡與管道施⼯，受惠
於市場技術人⼒缺乏，該業務毛利率較佳。 
公司在台灣彰濱設有主要生產基地與研發中心，並於中國浙江嘉興設有⼯廠及美國亞利桑那州設有子公司，截至
2Q26 地區別營收，台灣佔比 49.3%，為目前成⻑動能最強的區域，受惠於晶圓代⼯龍頭與特化廠強勁建廠需求，
台灣廠同時負責供應美洲及全球非中國市場、中國 44.8%，由中國廠獨立生產運作，專門供應中國內需半導體與
特化廠擴建需求、美國 3.4%，因當地晶圓廠建廠審核較慢且缺乏專業⼯程技術人⼒，推進時程較⻑，目前營收佔
比偏低、歐洲 1.5%、其他 1%。 
圖表、上品主要產品及產業概況

資料來源：玉山投顧、公司資料

| 營 運 概 況 
擺脫低谷舊單稀釋，產品組合高階化使毛利提升：1H26 合併營收 23.6 億元，YoY+4%，其中半導體產業佔營收
高達 91.3%，主要受惠全球晶圓代⼯龍頭與記憶體大廠積極擴產，現有特化廠產能無法滿足晶圓廠開出的需求，
帶動建廠與特化重化線設備強勁拉貨 ， 其餘為傳統石化產業占 6.4% ， 做為維持基礎⼯業需求的穩定貢獻 ， LCD 面
板與太陽能佔 1.2%，其他佔 1.1%，毛利率年減 1.7ppts 至 35.3%，下滑主因在於認列的營收中，相當大部分來
自 2025 年半導體與特化產業處於景氣低谷期時所接的舊訂單，因當時因市場需求薄弱、競爭較為激烈，產品平

### Page 3

3 
均毛利率較差且接單總量偏低，進而稀釋 1H26 整體毛利表現，其中 2Q26 單季毛利率季增 1.7ppts 至 35.6%，
顯示高毛利的訂單已逐漸回升，1H26 費用年增 25%至 3.25 億元，其中 2Q26 單季費用季增 4%至 1.65 億元，
主因包含公司為維持先進製程氟素設備的技術領先，研發支出佔營收比重較去年同期增加 1ppts，另外公司依據
會計準則提列了預期信用風險損失評估，約占費用的 2%，若扣除信用風險損失評估，實質營業費用率仍穩定維持
在 11-12%區間 ， 1H26 業外 0.44 億元 ， 主要來自利息收入增加 ， 以及美元與人⺠幣升值所帶來的匯兌收益 ， 2Q26
所得稅因集中認列未分配盈餘加徵 5%稅賦，有效稅率較 1Q26 拉升 7ppts 至 21.6%，稅後 EPS 5.63 元，3Q26
單季營收季增 22.5%至 16.0 億元，受惠於國內客戶持續建廠，年初在手訂單 38 億元轉為實質營收貢獻，且低毛
利訂單已消化完畢，推估毛利率進⼀步提升 1.38ppts 至 37.0%，稅後 EPS 4.46 元。

受惠全球 AI 市場需求與 ESG 回收政策，帶動氟素相關產品需求：截至八月底在手訂單已突破 75 億元，公司預估
2026 全年新接訂單將年增 91%，由於 AI 需求推升全球半導體晶圓廠展開大規模擴產，目前僅晶圓代⼯龍頭在台
灣與海外同時進行興建的晶圓廠就多達約 20 座，隨著客戶現場建廠與設備拉貨排程推進，主要電子級化學品相關
製造商均必須進行大規模擴產與建廠，另配合政府政策、ESG 環保法規，中鼎(9933)等大型⼯程統包商均跨入回
收領域，直接推升氟素內襯設備與管道系統的需求，化學品運輸槽⾞因應晶圓廠大量使用化學品，預估接單量將
突破 1,000 台，相較去年 600 台是翻倍成⻑，目前槽⾞設備的訂單交期已往後推三年陸續交貨，客戶嚴格把關進
程，確保原料與槽⾞交期不致拖累建廠進度，由於產出的大型槽體與設備體積龐大，產品製造完成入庫後，必須
待客戶建廠空間準備就緒並通知拉貨才能認列營收，因此單季成⻑趨勢比單月數據更為準確，預估 2026 全年營
收年增 18%至 55.8 億元，在成本與毛利率方面，上游氟素樹脂原料，如大金(Daikin)、科慕(Chemours)，全球出
現嚴重供不應求，交期⻑達 8 個月以上，供需缺口約在 5-10%，且面臨 PFAS 環保審查與附加稅費增加的壓⼒，
但公司為原料廠的⼀線直接採購客戶，享有優先供料保障，並具備從原始樹脂顆粒自製半成品(板材/管材)到終端
設備的能⼒，另外由於公司產品設備外殼高達 90%為白鐵(不鏽鋼)，透過高市佔率優勢，能將每⼀筆新接訂單動
態重新計價轉嫁給客戶來降低成本壓⼒，預期在高毛利的產品訂單陸續開出，使整體毛利率回升 3.6ppts 至
36.58%，稅前盈餘成⻑ 40%至 14.4 億元，稅後 EPS 14.45 元。

產能擴充，持續投入高階新產品開發：2027 年預期營收受惠高能見度槽⾞與建廠設備大批量交付認列，高單價
與高毛利設備將於明年進入出貨高峰，另外新擴充的產能部分，以槽⾞為單位，中國嘉興廠進行 20%的產能提
升、動線優化與組裝委外調整，台灣廠已從每月 80 台提升至 140 台，皆於 1Q27 開始貢獻，台灣廠預計明年規
劃再提升 20%至 170 台，有效紓解目前產能滿載的壓⼒，支撐明年營收大幅成⻑。 
公司持續投入新產品研發，其中離型膜應用於晶圓廠高溫區環境的關鍵新產品，已開始小量出貨，預計於 2026
年底完成送樣，明年貢獻營收，高純度配件與包材，包含關閉時僅留 90% 殘留的射出件接頭、100L ⼀體成型
槽⾞杯、新型取樣器、濾芯過濾產品等，隨著客戶認證通過，高毛利產品出貨比重將在明年進⼀步拉高，⼯程業
務高毛利貢獻持續挹注，主要因應特化廠建廠與回收系統需求，化學廠內部的管道與襯裡⼯程訂單持續增加，在
⼯程人⼒極度缺乏的市場環境下，公司內部⼯程團隊提供整合性服務，使⼯程業務維持高毛利率表現，持續挹注
明年獲利，由於同業競爭對手大多採取保守態度不敢大幅投資，公司在地化供應優勢與積極擴產與技術升級策
略，預估市佔率將持續攀升，有助於毛利率表現。 
另因應 AI 與高頻高速傳輸技術的突破，如 PTFE/氟素樹脂與銅箔結合的低介電常數與低介電損耗(Low Dk/Df)特
性需求迫切，公司與特定下游廠商合作，進行高黏性 PTFE 氟素基板材料與金屬銅箔結合的開發與測試，由於技
術非常高，氟素樹脂化學惰性極強，提升其高黏著性並與銅箔緊密貼合，同時保持極佳的高頻電性，屬於材料科
學上的高難度突破，目前開發進度尚未正式通過客戶驗證，仍處於配方調整測試階段，屬中⻑期高毛利產品線。

### Page 4

4 
預估 2027 全年成⻑ 25%至 69.7 億元，受惠兩岸新產能開出、高毛利槽⾞與高階新產品比重提升，毛利率提升
2.92ppts 至 39.5%，費用率因公司擴充產能主要仰賴人員技術培訓、廠區動線優化與前後段⼯序，非大幅興建
廠房，因此未來不會產生龐大折舊費用來墊高費用率，隨營業收入規模擴大而稀釋，預期維持在 11.8%，營益率
提升 3.6ppts 至 27.7%，稅前盈餘年成⻑ 40.8%至 20.2 億元，稅後 EPS 19.84 元。

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部對緯穎(6669)維持買進建議，主係：(1) 3Q26營收季增56%，
大幅優於預期；(2) 4Q26在AWS的ASIC氣冷出貨帶動下，預期4Q營
收季增個位數 ；(3) 雖T3水冷機櫃短期遞延， 但預估27年逐月攀升，
公司仍為主要供應商。(4) AMD MI450 於1H27年放量，客戶與平台
更加多元；(5) 新增之Oracle VR200預期的訂單約2.5K，約在27年
貢獻營收。 綜上所述，研究部上修 2026/2027 年營收至1.46兆元
/3.07兆元(原估1.27兆元/2.38兆元)，EPS上修至143.16元/241.16
元，目前評價僅8~9倍，位於低檔，給予12.5倍本益比，目標價3010
元(2027EPS*12.5PER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收大幅優於預期 
緯穎9月營收為1,726億元，月增19.6%，年增100%，整體3Q26
營收達4,346億元 ，季增56.2%，年增63%，營收達成率達到
135%(研究部預估)/128%(市場共識)， 大幅優於預期 ， 主係Meta
通用型及AWS T3氣冷出貨旺盛所致。3Q26財報尚未公布，研究
部上修至毛利率8.9%(原估8.1%)， 季減0.4pts， 優於原先預期 ，
主係通用伺服器占比較高， 產品組合較佳 ，對毛利率形成支撐，
預估3Q EPS約45.56元。

2. 4Q營運動能可望延續 
展望4Q26，雖水冷機櫃須遞延至年底至1H27才會逐漸放量 ，但
仍預期T3氣冷機櫃及通用持續出貨，預估4Q26營收約4,666億
元，季增7.4%，年增59.5%。毛利率8.2% (原估7.3%)，季減
0.7pts， 主係高單價ASIC水冷機櫃占比尚低， 預估EPS約45.18
元。整體而言，2H26在ASIC氣冷及通用伺服器帶動下，營運明
顯優於上半年

3. T3短期雜音不改長期趨勢，2027年水冷逐步放量 
根據供應鏈調查：(1) T3水冷機櫃初期產線磨合不順，客戶因
此下修今年出貨規劃。不過，2027年水冷機櫃出貨規劃仍逐季
攀升，在晶片端的水冷比重也提升 至50~70%，單價為氣冷3-4
倍以上，成為2027年營收成長的主要動能。在AWS下一代T4產
品，公司持續參與機櫃設計，有望採用全水冷設計，預期28年
出貨，為中長期成長動能。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

(2)MI450機櫃部分，Meta專案集中在2027年出貨，單價約450
～500萬美元，訂單逐漸明確，預估約可達5～7K櫃。(3) 新增
Oracle VR200訂單，預期約2.5K櫃，約在27年開始貢獻營收。
公司在GPU伺服器的客戶版圖進一步擴大。

4. 目前評價位於低檔，維持買進建議 
綜上所述，考量各客戶訂單逐漸明確， 研究部上修2026/2027
年營收至1.46兆元/3.07兆元(原估1.27兆元/2.38兆元)，2027
年毛利率雖然因高單價水冷機櫃及GPU機櫃比重提升，下滑至
6.5%(原估7.1%)， 但營收規模大幅擴張 ， 獲利仍可望顯著成長 。
股本部分，緯穎配發股票股利，除權基準日為9/8，配股率達
1.9828倍，股本由18.69億元增至56.02億元，EPS因此依比例
稀釋，故2026/2027年稀釋後EPS上修至143.16元/241.16元。
目前評價僅8~9倍，位於低檔，給予12倍本益比，目標價3010
元(2027EPS*12.5PER)。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

3Q26 Review: 악재의 중첩,

투자의견 BUY

6개월 목표주가 1,640,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
739,000 
방위산업

◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
◼ 컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
◼ 4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
4Q26E-2Q27E 지상방산 및 항공우주 EBITDA에 Target 12MF EV/EBITDA 25.0x 
적용, 상장 자회사 가치 SOTP 통해 목표주가 산출. 3Q26 실적 아쉬우나 투자포
인트에는 변함 없어 멀티플은 변경 없이 적용. 27년 실적 추정치 상향에 따라 본
업 가치 상승, 26년 스페인 자주포 양산 및 미국 자주포 시제 사업자로 선정되며 
현지화 전략이 유효했음을 증명. 3Q26 실적에 대한 우려를 소화하며 글로벌 피어
들과의 멀티플 격차 축소를 전망함

컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 6.1조원(-6.0% YoY), 영업이익 4,520억원(-46.2% YoY, 
OPM 7.4%)으로 컨센서스인 매출액 7.4조원 및 영업이익 1.1조원을 크게 하회
하는 실적 시현. 하회의 주요 요인으로는 3Q26 필리조선소 적자폭 확대, 지상
방산 부문 실적이 부진한 영향. 지상방산 부문의 경우 매출액 1.2조원(-41.0% 
YoY), 영업이익 220억원(-96.2% YoY, OPM 1.7%)을 시현했는데, 폴란드 관련 
매출이 부재했던 1Q26 영업이익률 16.9%보다도 낮은 실적을 보임. 이는 국내
외 사업 모두에서 악재가 겹쳐 발생했던 것으로 보이는데 국내 사업에서는 마
진율이 낮은 사업이 매출로 인식되었고 수출 사업에서는 폴란드 매출 부재 뿐 
아니라 이집트, 호주 K9 자주포 매출 인식 또한 예상 대비 부진해 고정비 레버
리지 효과로 3Q26 영업이익률이 크게 훼손된 것으로 추정. 이외 대전사업장 복
구에 따른 비용, 기타 사업 충당금 등 총 500억원 수준의 일회성 비용이 발생했
으며 영업외손익에서는 원화 강세에 따른 환손실이 주로 반영되었음

4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향 
1Q26에서 3Q26까지 이뤄지지 않았던 폴란드 EC2 K9 자주포 30문 인도는 
4Q26에 집중될 것으로 보이며 폴란드 EC1 천무 또한 기존 가이던스였던 26년 
천무 40대에서 60대로 인도 대수 상향이 이뤄짐. 이에 따라 4Q26 지상방산 영
업이익률은 22.8%로 회복을 전망. 27년에는 폴란드 K9 자주포 및 천무 인도 대
수 증가로 26년 수출 비중 58.0%에서 77.2%로 증가, 이에 따라 영업이익률 또
한 매출 믹스 개선에 따라 19.8%에서 28.0%로 개선을 전망

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5

Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,

credit persisting.

NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation

NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Turning point?
• We expect the unemployment rate to edge
down to 5.5% for the September quarter.
• Job numbers have been gaining momentum
in recent months, and now look to be enough
to outstrip population growth. However, the
participation rate will be the deciding factor.
• Despite cost-of-living pressures on workers,
the existing slack in the labour market is likely
to keep wage growth subdued.
• Our forecasts are a little more positive than

what the RBNZ assumed in their September
policy statement. Assuming they deliver on
an OCR hike this afternoon, the labour market
surveys could have an important bearing on
whether they follow up with another hike in
December.

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of Q3 labour market surveys:
4 November, 10:45am.
28 Oct 2026 | Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.

A SHOCK-RESISTANT EXPANSION
US growth remains resilient despite successive shocks. According to our forecasts, it is expected to reach 2.2% in 2026 and
2.3% in 2027. Its main driver is still investment in artificial intelligence (AI), which has remained entirely unaffected by the
energy crisis. The labour market has seen a marked improvement in job creation and a fall in the unemployment rate. Core

inflation remains stubbornly high and above the Federal Reserve (Fed)’s target. Against this backdrop, the Fed has begun a
hiking cycle in September, which, according to our scenario, would bring the Fed Funds target to 4.50% (upper limit) by Q1
2027. At the same time, long-term rates are under upward pressure.
1  This includes: energy and communications; data centres (facilities); IT equipment and peripherals; communication equipment (equipment); software; and research and develop-

ment (intellectual-property products).
THE ENERGY SHOCK IS NOT HOLDING BACK GROWTH
Driven by AI-related investment, US growth is holding up despite ri -
sing energy inflation. We forecast GDP growth at 2.2% in 2026 and
2.3% in 2027 on an annual average basis. The AI-related investment
cycle1 (+14.8% y/y in H1 2026) is expected to continue, accompanied by
potential productivity gains. However, other private-investment seg -
ments (including residential property) remain sluggish. In addition, the

rising cost of capital could accentuate its ‘K-shaped’ profile. Household
consumption is expected, on the whole, to benefit from the rebound in
job creation and to remain above 2% y/y through to the end of 2027.
In H1 2026, it held up against resurging inflation due to an average
year-on-year increase of 11.5% in tax refunds and a savings rate stan-
ding at its lowest level since 2022 (2.8% in Q2).
RECORD CAPITAL-RAISING DRIVEN BY AI
This year, AI-related investments are driving massive capital-raising

by companies through loans, bond issues or share placements. This
trend is set to continue given the seemingly colossal scale of the fun -
ding requirements, even as risk premia in the bond market begin to
widen. According to their statements, the five tech giants could invest
nearly USD 900 bn this year and over USD 1,000 bn next year—more
than double the figure for 2025. Spurred on by regulatory easing, the
major US banks have announced that they will mobilise significant re-

sources to support projects considered strategic for the United States
(AI, defence, energy and infrastructure). While funding for the AI boom
appears unaffected by rising long-term interest rates, these latter are
likely to continue to weigh on household demand for mortgages. Since
the start of the conflict in the Middle East, 30-year mortgage rates
have risen by 78 bp (to 6.76% at the start of September). Conversely,
consumer lending remained buoyant in H1 2026 and is supporting

household spending.
TWO-SPEED GROWTH IN INDUSTRY
Industrial production rebounded slightly in 2025 (+1.1%), driven by AI
and aerospace. We forecast an acceleration to +1.5% in 2026 and +2%
in 2027. Investment in AI is bolstering the electronics sector, while the
(civil and military) aerospace sector is rebounding as order backlogs
are cleared and defence requirements rise. Conversely, sectors ex -
posed to rising tariffs (in particular, the steel, aluminium and auto -

motive sectors) are still on the back burner, contrary to the objective
of these measures. This positive momentum in industrial production,
while concentrated, is expected to continue, bolstered by the recove -
ry in business sentiment. The ISM Manufacturing Index has been in
expansionary territory since early 2026, despite ongoing pressures on
prices and supply chains.

LABOUR MARKET: A LOW UNEMPLOYMENT RATE ON A DOWNWARD
TREND
Job creation (non-farm payrolls) has rebounded, averaging +80,000 per
month since the start of the year, compared with +10,000 in 2025. This
growth is no longer limited to the healthcare sector. Excluding health-
care, job creation is up by an average of +38,000 in 2026, compared

with -47,000 in 2025. Manufacturing and construction, two sectors
driven by AI, are contributing to this rebound. At the same time, the
labour force has not grown since January 2025 due to the decline in the
foreign-born workforce (down 4.2% cumulatively over the period). Ex -
cluding the impact of COVID-19, the labour force participation rate is at
its lowest level since the 1970s, standing at around 61.5%. The rebound
in labour demand and persistent pressure on the labour supply have

brought the unemployment rate down to 4.1% in August 2026 (a 0.3 pp
fall since the start of the year). The jobless rate is expected to fall to
3.9% at the end of 2027 (4.2% corresponds to full employment, accor -
ding to the CBO) thanks to a job creation rate of around 40,000–60,000
per month.
INFLATION: STRUCTURALLY ABOVE TARGET
The rise in oil prices has triggered a rebound in CPI inflation. The
contribution from the energy component (+1.2 pp) largely accounts for

the rise in inflation since February. A slowdown was seen (from +4.2%
to +3.4% y/y) between the May peak and August. This is expected to
continue due to base effects on energy prices. We anticipate the trough
to be +1.8% y/y in Q2 2027 before a stabilisation at around +2.3% y/y
later in the year.
Meanwhile, core inflation is expected to remain above the Fed’s 2%
target, and above +2.5% y/y in 2027. This highlights the structural
nature of inflation, beyond short-term fluctuations linked to energy.

Non-housing services are expected to show significant inertia, with in-
flation hovering at around +3% y/y by the end of 2027. The potential for
disinflation also appears limited for housing-related services, as the
rise in rents shows no signs of abating. Finally, inflation on non-energy
goods is slowing as the effects of tariffs fade. However, the AI cycle is
exerting upward pressure through the general rise in the cost of elec -
tronic goods.
FED: GREEN LIGHT FOR RATE HIKES

The FOMC has embarked on a cycle of rate increases. The Fed Funds
target was raised to 4.0% (+25 bp) at the September meeting, and we
anticipate two further rises that would bring the policy rate to 4.5% in
January 2027. These three rises (made possible by reassuring develop-
ments in employment and economic activity) are intended to reverse
the three cuts in 2025 adopted to counter the ‘downside risks’ to the
labour market at that time. As a result, the committee can recalibrate

its policy towards ‘price stability’ and, through its interest rate deci -
sions, reflect the priority given to this objective in its communications.
The risks point towards further monetary tightening: in our baseline
scenario, core inflation would remain sticky and the fall in unemploy -
ment would give the FOMC room for manoeuvre to raise rates further.
PUBLIC FINANCES: UNDERLYING DETERIORATION AND PRESSURE ON
LONG- TERM RATES
According to our estimates, the federal deficit is set to widen by 0.7 pp

to -6.6% of GDP in 2026 (by 0.4 pp to -3.1% for the primary deficit),
weighed down by falling tariff revenue (refunds of tariffs already col -
lected and lower-than-expected receipts). In 2027, the end of this one-
off effect is expected to lead to a marginal improvement to -6.3% (to
-2.8% for the primary deficit). The growing burden of interest payments
(3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 3.6% in 2027, following 3.2% in 2025) and
the conflict in Iran (affecting the military budget) are weighing on the

budget balance and federal-debt dynamics. Federal debt is still on an
upward trend, with no particular change in direction, exceeding 100% of
GDP (101.9%, up 3.3 pp, in 2026, and 103.5% in 2027). It is expected to
reach 110% by the end of the decade against a backdrop of budget de-
ficits of close to 6% of GDP , assuming fiscal policy remains unchanged.
Furthermore, the mid-term elections (which see the entire House of
Representatives and one-third of the Senate up for re-election) could

result in a divided Congress or in both chambers changing hands to
the Democratic Party. Should the former occur, this would increase the
risk of a legislative deadlock, while the presidential veto would limit
the Democrats’ ability to influence fiscal policy in the latter scenario.
Against this backdrop, pressure on the bond market is likely to continue
in the coming months. Treasury issuance is rising, fuelled by increa -
sing spending requirements (in areas such as AI, defence and supply

chains). At the same time, shifts in investor demographics are increa-
sing demand volatility, and issuances by companies in the artificial
intelligence sector are intensifying competition for capital. We antici -
pate a 10-year yield of 4.95% in Q1 2027, before a slight easing brought
about by Fed rate rises, which would lend credibility to the price-sta -
bility objective. However, according to our forecast, 10-year yield is
expected to remain close to a 20-year high, at 4.80% in Q4 2027.

THE US RELIES ON IMPORTS FOR ITS GROWTH
The US trade deficit has recently started to rise again. According to
our forecasts, it is expected to account for the same proportion of GDP
in 2026 as it did in 2024 (4.4%). This trend is driven by imports of
AI-related capital goods, which are set to rise from 1.2% to 2.1% of GDP
between 2022 and 2026. Investment in AI is, in fact, import-intensive
(Chart 2), which dampens its positive impact on growth and highlights

the US’s dependence on foreign supplies in a cycle that it is neverthe-
less spearheading.
The additional tariffs have failed to reverse the trend in the trade de -
ficit. Furthermore, the IEEPA tariffs struck down by the Supreme Court
were replaced, in July 2026, by ‘Section 301’ tariffs (forced labour) ran-
ging from 10% to 12.5%. These tariffs target 60 countries and have
been introduced on top of existing sector-specific tariffs (steel, alumi -

nium and automotive). Following these adjustments, the statutory rate
(measured against the 2024 import structure) remained slightly above
10%. The main risks relate to potential new sector-specific tariffs and
the renegotiation of the USMCA.

MAIN POINTS:
1. The core PCE index increased +0.25% in August, somewhat below expectations, and
the year-over-year rate increased 3.01%. We estimate that the Dallas Fed’s trimmed
mean PCE measure increased 0.16% month over month and 2.20% year over year.
2. Year-over-year core PCE was revised downward 0.36pp in July to 2.98%, primarily
reflecting the methodological revisions to the calculation of the portfolio management
(-0.23pp), computer accessories and software (-0.11pp), and legal services (+0.01pp)

components of PCE. These downward revisions were larger than we expected, mostly
reflecting a larger revision to the portfolio management component than we expected.
3. Today’s inflation report, especially the downward revisions resulting from the
methodological changes, imply that core PCE inflation is likely to substantially
undershoot the median FOMC participant’s forecast of 3.4% for 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis.
We now expect a 3.0% increase for core PCE in 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis. Coupled with

comments from New York Fed President John Williams yesterday that “with the policy
action we took at our September meeting, there is no need for urgency, and we have
time to gather more information,” we now think that an October hike is unlikely.  We are
pushing back the second hike in our forecast to December, and we see a strong chance
that the FOMC will ultimately conclude that additional rate hikes are unnecessary.
4. Real GDP was revised up by 0.7pp to +2.2% in Q2 (quarter-over-quarter annualized),

largely reflecting upward revisions to consumption (+0.4pp to +3.8%) and investment
(+1.9pp to +4.6%) growth. The strength in consumer spending in Q2 likely continued to
reflect the boost to consumer cashflow from larger-than-usual tax refunds, but we
continue to expect consumer spending growth to slow somewhat in 2026Q4 as the fiscal
support fades and the headwind from higher energy prices remains. Stronger
investment growth reflected an upward revision to private fixed investment (+0.5pp to

+9.0%), driven by data center investment in the nonresidential structures category
(+1.9pp to +0.1%), and an upward revision to the contribution from inventories (+0.2pp
to -0.5pp).
5. Reflecting today’s annual revisions to the national accounts, real GDP was revised up
by 0.4pp to +2.5% in Q1 (quarter-over-quarter annualized), largely reflecting upward
revisions to the contributions from consumption and exports. Revisions for prior years
were modest: real GDP growth on a Q4/Q4 basis was unrevised for 2025 (+2.0%) and

2023 (+3.4%), and slightly revised down for 2024 (-0.2pp to +2.4%) and up for 2022
30 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs

### Page 3

(+0.1pp to +1.3%). 
6. Personal income increased 0.2% in August, below expectations. Year-over-year 
disposable personal income growth reached 4.8% in August. Personal spending rose 
0.9% in August, in line with consensus expectations. The savings rate decreased 0.5pp to 
4.1% in August, after a large 1.6pp upward revision to the savings rate last month driven 
by an upward revision to the level of disposable personal income (+1.2pp contribution) 
and a downward revision to personal spending as a share of DPI (+0.4pp). 
7. According to the Advance Economic Indicators Report, the goods trade deficit 
widened by $13.7bn to $132.6bn (seasonally adjusted) in August, well above 
expectations. Goods exports increased by $3.7bn to $203.4bn, largely driven by an 
increase in industrial supplies exports. Goods imports increased by $17.4bn to 
$336.1bn, driven by a large increase in industrial supplies and capital goods imports. 
8. According to the ADP report, private sector employment rose by 90k in September 
(month-over-month, seasonally adjusted), slightly above consensus expectations. 
Employment in the services sector rose 59k, primarily reflecting a 55k increase in 
education and health services and a 22k increase in leisure and hospitality employment 
that were partly offset by a 16k decrease in financial activities employment. 
Employment in goods-producing industries increased by 31k, driven by a 17k increase in 
manufacturing and a 15k increase in construction employment. ADP’s new median 
annual base pay measure, which better tracks other measures of wage growth than its 
gross pay measure, increased 3.0% for job stayers, unchanged from the August pace, 
and 4.8% for job changers, 0.1pp above the August pace. We left our forecast for August 
nonfarm payroll growth unchanged at +80k (vs. +90k consensus) ahead of Friday’s 
release. 
9. The details of the personal income and spending report were firmer than our previous 
GDP tracking assumptions. While the trade-related details of the Advance Economic 
Indicators report were much weaker than our previous assumptions, suggesting notably 
stronger imports growth in Q3 than we previously assumed, the inventories details were 
significantly stronger. On net, we lowered our Q3 GDP tracking estimate by 0.1pp to 
+3.3% (quarter-over-quarter annualized).

Document 432: “黑色星期五”的导火索：Anthropic只能“垃圾级”？

Source PDF: all report pdf/“黑色星期五”的导火索：Anthropic只能“垃圾级”？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-31
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

投资级评级能让 Anthropic 以更低利率借钱，为这些项⽬融资。⽽很多保险公司、养⽼基⾦
按规定只能投资级为主，或者限制持有垃圾级债务的规模。主体⼀旦掉到 BB+ 及以下，它
能触及的资⾦池将明显收窄，利率也会更⾼。
摘要：
⿊⽯告诉投资者， Anthropic 上市后可能⽆法⽴即获得投资级评级，预计主要评级机构将给予其
“ 较⾼档 ” 的垃圾级评级。
据英国《⾦融时报》援引知情⼈⼠报道称，⿊⽯向 Anthropic 最新⼀笔 630 亿美元芯⽚融资交易的
投资者传达了这⼀信息。若评级落在垃圾级， Anthropic 为逾 8000 亿美元的算⼒计划融资前景将
更加复杂。
美国财经博主、 YouTube 频道 Meet Kevin 主理⼈ Kevin Paffrath 认为，这则消息是周五美股加速
下跌的导⽕索： “ 消息⼀出，市场跌得更狠了。 ” 在他看来，症结在于 Anthropic 的算⼒承诺需要⼗
⼏年的利润才能覆盖，偏偏⼜拿不到投资级评级。
当天，亚⻢逊和英特尔的财报已加剧市场对 AI 热潮可持续性的担忧，美股在报道发布前就已⼤幅

⾛低；报道发布后，美国科技股进⼀步跌⾄⽇内低点，纳斯达克 100 指数收跌 3.5% 。
投资级预期落空
知情⼈⼠称，过去⼏个⽉，⿊⽯⼀直告诉投资伙伴，预计 Anthropic 将在年底前拿到投资级评
级。但根据主要评级机构给出的指引，⿊⽯近期改⼝称， Anthropic 获得 BB 区间或以上评级的可
能性更⼤。
按标普和惠誉的评级体系， BBB- 及以上为投资级， BB+ 及以下为投机级，市场俗称 “ 垃圾级 ” 。
BB 区间（ BB+ 、 BB 、 BB- ）是垃圾级中最⾼的⼀档，紧邻投资级⻔槛，这正是 “ 较⾼档 ” 垃圾级
的含义。
这⼀评级对 Anthropic ⾄关重要。公司周⼀提交上市申请，下⽉可能完成有史以来规模最⼤的
IPO 。招股书勾勒了⼀项 “ ⼤胆但理性 ” （ bold but sane ）的计划：从现在到 2045 年，在算⼒基础
设施上投⼊逾 8000 亿美元。
⽬前， Anthropic 持有逾 1120 亿美元现⾦及等价物，投资者预计 IPO 还将募资约 1000 亿美元。

投资级评级能让 Anthropic 以更低利率借钱，为这些项⽬融资。⽽很多保险公司、养⽼基⾦按规
定只能投资级为主，或者限制持有垃圾级债务的规模。主体⼀旦掉到 BB+ 及以下，它能触及的资
⾦池将明显收窄，利率也会更⾼。
“ 关键在于能否获得信贷，这⼀点⾮常重要 …… 如果（ Anthropic ）不是投资级，信贷成本就会上
升，这对谁都不是好事。 ” ⼀位专注美国科技股的基⾦经理说。对于当天的股市抛售，这位基⾦经
理的评价是： “ 外⾯简直是⼀场⾎洗。 ”
Anthropic 和竞争对⼿ OpenAI 的银⾏家都在游说⼏家最⼤的评级机构，希望两家 AI 公司上市后获
得投资级评级。对企业借贷成本影响更⼤的三⼤评级机构 —— 惠誉、穆迪和标普，⾄今尚未正
式给予 Anthropic 任何信⽤评级。
账⾯不差，承诺太重
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
在 Kevin Paffrath 看来， Anthropic 问题不在资产负债表。
据他解读， Anthropic 持有约 1120 亿美元现⾦和 190 亿美元短期投资，合计约 1310 亿美元，流动
负债仅约 70 亿美元；经营现⾦流已经转正，上半年⾃由现⾦流约 35 亿美元。若 IPO 再募资 1000
亿美元，可动⽤资⾦将达到约 2310 亿美元。
压⼒来⾃承诺的规模。Paffrath 测算，约 8000 亿美元的算⼒承诺扣除上述资⾦后，仍有约 5690
亿美元需要填补。假设 Anthropic 年底年化营收达到 800 亿美元、净利率为 40% ，每年约可贡献
320 亿美元，覆盖这⼀缺⼝需要约 17 年。
“ 相当于把 17 年的利润都押在了未来的算⼒承诺上，这才是让⼈紧张的地⽅。 ” 他说， “ 刚拿到垃圾

级评级，你要怎么做到？这就是今天让市场恼⽕的原因。 ”
Paffrath 还拿 SpaceX 作对⽐。他提到， SpaceX 已获得三⼤评级机构的投资级评级，原因可能
在于其业务不只依赖 AI ，还有⽕箭发射和星链。
不过， Paffrath 本⼈依然看多。他认为 Anthropic 的营收增⻓将超出市场预期；对于外界担忧的
供应商融资，他也不以为意，理由是博通净利率约 55% ，即便变相把芯⽚ “ 送 ” 给 Anthropic 也⾜
以覆盖成本。他将此轮下跌视为逢低买⼊的机会。
“ 北极星 ” ：借博通的信⽤融资
这笔 630 亿美元的融资代号 “ 北极星 ” （ Project North Star ），华尔街银⾏已于本周早些时候完成交
易。资⾦将⽤于 Anthropic 租赁⾕歌 1.5 吉⽡的芯⽚。⿊⽯担任锚定投资者，并与出资银⾏合作，
将债务转售给其他投资者。
交易结构显⽰出 Anthropic 对供应商信⽤的依赖。逾 420 亿美元优先级贷款由博通担保，博通参与
联合开发这批芯⽚，拥有 A 档⾼位的投资级评级，这是 Anthropic 能够拿到这笔融资的重要因素。

机构投资者⻘睐优先级债务，因为⼀旦违约，其受偿顺序排在次级贷款之前。
另有 180 亿美元次级债务以 Anthropic 的租赁付款为⽀撑，贷款⼈因此更直接地承担 Anthropic 的
信⽤⻛险。据《⾦融时报》此前报道，⿊⽯旗下基⾦以超过 10% 的⾼收益率认购了其中 90 亿美
元。
这部分次级债务获得 Kroll Bond Rating Agency （ KBRA ）给予的 BB+ 评级（仅⽐投资级低⼀个
⼦级），展望为正⾯，较四个⽉前类似交易 “ ⼤天空 ” （ Big Sky ）的稳定展望略有改善；晨星 DBRS
则给予 BB 评级。
Anthropic 、⿊⽯、 KBRA 、晨星 DBRS 和标普均拒绝置评，惠誉和穆迪未回复置评请求。

《醫療器械喜憂參半，但必要性手術需求仍佳》
近月醫療服務設備股為健護中股價表現較差 ，主因：
1)亞培受到產品責任險達成部分和解協議 ，後續還
有其他聯邦 、州判決仍為股價壓力； 2)波士頓科學
受資安事件影響生產宣告無法達成財測 ，HRS的左

心耳封堵術 (LAAC) 指南適用 WATCHMAN、 亞培
Amulet，可同時做消融+LAAC，但LAAC不能完全
替代口服抗凝血藥物 (OAC)對預防中風的效用 ，仍
為二線，等同喜憂參半 。另受自付成本增加 ，預期
擇期、外科手術需求疲軟延伸至年底 ，機器人手術
成長率區間接近 14.5%，下半年低於上半年 ，將以
擴大翻新系統的布署降低門檻及增加門診手術需求 ，
擴大目標市場 ，但必要醫療手術需求健康 ，心血管
類手術下半年仍成長，醫療器材基本面喜憂參半。
《星級獎金與醫療成本控管力使長期獲利分化》
每月聯邦醫療保險優勢計畫投保人數成長趨勢持續 ，
CMS預計於2026年10月中公布 2027年醫療優勢計
畫(MA)星級評等，因評級變嚴將影響醫保公司會員
留存率、競爭力及2028年品質獎金收入不確定性增
加，低於4星級業者面臨品質獎金減少與長期獲利承
壓風險。另無意外法案獨立爭議解決 (IDR)仲裁成本
持續推升商業保險理賠支出 ，增加2027年雇主團體
及ACA市場保費定價壓力 。管理式醫療業者今年展

現利潤優先於市占率、砍不賺錢計畫/人數拉抬利潤
率，但明年若保費調漲幅度不及醫療成本增速 ，恐
推升醫療損失率 、壓縮承保利潤 。後續獲利關鍵在
於星級評等 、保費調整能力及理賠成本控管 ，業者
間的長期獲利表現可能進一步分化。

Document 442: 【國泰證期研究部】半導體產業-400G lane驅動下一代光調變與封裝升級-20261021

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】半導體產業-400G lane驅動下一代光調變與封裝升級-20261021.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

1 
產業更新 – 半導體產業 
400G/lane 驅動下一代光調變與封裝升級 
What’s New - TFLN 邁入量產並開啟 400G/lane 封裝新週期 
TFLN 由技術驗證跨入商業量產階段，HyperLight、聯電(2303 TT)及聯穎光電共同擴大 TFLN 量
產平台，其中聯穎 6 吋產線已完成客戶驗證，聯電則導入 8 吋。產品端亦由 200G/lane 快速往
400G/lane 升級。相較矽基調變器在高速化後面臨頻寬、插入損耗及驅動電壓間的取捨，TFLN
利用 Pockels Effect具備高頻寬、低光損及低驅動電壓優勢，並有望降低獨立射頻Driver 需求。
隨 TFLN 頻寬提升至 100~150GHz 以上，封裝介面逐漸成為新瓶頸，開始導入 Flip-Chip 及凸
塊接合以縮短 Driver-to-Modulator 電性路徑，Gold Bump 亦有望受惠異質整合需求增加。 
Trends/Outlook - 高速光互連進入材料分工並帶動調變器升級 
展望 27~30 年，我們認為 TFLN 不會全面取代矽光子，而將優先切入對頻寬、功耗及訊號品質
要求較高的高速光調變器，形成 Si/SiN 負責被動光路、TFLN 負責主動調變、III-V 負責雷射及
CMOS 負責訊號處理的異質整合架構。26~27 年 1.6T/200G per lane 仍以矽光子為主流，
27~29 年進入 3.2T/400G per lane 後，TFLN 憑藉 130~150GHz 以上頻寬、低 Vπ 及低插入
損耗優勢有望加速滲透。隨產業由 6 吋往 8 吋 TFLN 量產發展，競爭焦點將逐步轉向成本、良
率及高速封裝能力 ， 並帶動Flip-Chip、RDL、Cu Pillar 及 Gold Bump 等晶圓級互連需求提升 。

Investment Summary 
1. TFLN 產業正處於由高性能元件走向可量產平台的關鍵轉折期，投資主軸可分為前段晶圓製
造與後段高速互連。聯電(2303 TT) 已正式加入 HyperLight 與聯穎的 TFLN 量產體系，負責將
既有 6 吋平台進一步擴展至 8 吋，同時公司本身亦具備 12 吋矽光子量產與 400G/lane 純矽平
台布局，因此並非單壓 TFLN 取代 Silicon，而是同時掌握 Si 與 TFLN 兩條技術路線，可在
200G/lane 維持矽光子量產優勢，並於 400G/lane 以上參與 TFLN 及 Si/TFLN 異質整合。 
2. 頎邦(6147 TT)則位於 TFLN 後段高速互連環節 ， 公司目前已透過矽光子切入PD、TIA、Driver
及 Modulator，並具備 Gold Bump 、Cu Pillar、RDL、晶圓測試及切割等完整後段能力。隨
TFLN 由 6 吋往 8 吋量產擴張 ， 以及400G/lane 以上產品提高 Driver-to-Modulator Flip-Chip
滲透率，頎邦既有晶圓級 Bumping 與互連平台有望直接受惠。
